8
Conclusions

In this paper, we have introduced a factor network autoregression (FNAR) for time series characterized by multiple network effects. Estimation is based on two steps. First, we extract few network factors common across the layers of the underlying multilayer network. Second, we estimate a factor-augmented NAR or FNAR where the network effects are determined by the latent network factors. The FNAR errors are allowed to have an underlying factor structure capturing common correlations across the network nodes. We prove consistency and asymptotic normality of the proposed estimators as the number of layers, nodes and time observations diverges to infinity.

The results of an empirical application show that, by accounting for cross-country economic and financial linkages, the model provides a rich description of the dynamics of GDP growth rates and produces accurate forecasts.

We outline three possible extensions of this work, which we leave for further research. First, by adapting the works by
Chen et al. 2023
and
Zhu et al. 2023
to the FNAR framework, we could consider a FNAR with momentum and nodal coefficients which are group specific, where the group structure is unknown and the number of groups
K
can grow with the number of nodes
N
.
Second, by generalizing to the tensor setting the three-pass regression filter by
Kelly and Pruitt 2015
,
we could improve the performance of our estimator by accounting also for the information contained in the vector of dependent variables
y_{t}
when extracting the network factors. Third, by extending to the tensor case an approach similar to the one proposed by
Wu and Zhou 2024
, we could allow for time-varying network factor loadings under the standard assumption of local stationarity.

Factor Network Autoregressions

Supplementary material

Matteo Barigozzi    Giuseppe Cavaliere    Graziano Moramarco

This supplemental material contains several appendices to our paper.
In Appendix
Estimation of node factors
we describe how to estimate the node factors by Principal Components Analysis (PCA) on the FNAR residuals.
In Appendices
OLS estimator and its asymptotic properties
and
GLS estimator and its asymptotic properties
, we introduce the OLS estimator and a GLS-type estimator of the FNAR coefficients. In Appendix
Proofs of the main results
, we provide the proofs for Propositions and Theorems. In Appendix
Auxiliary lemmata
, we provide and prove all auxiliary results.
In Appendix
Monte Carlo simulations
we provide additional simulation results.
In Appendix
Empirical application: data and additional results
we provide additional information on the data used in our empirical application, as well as additional empirical results.
Finally, in Appendix
NAR estimation based on full Tucker decompositions
we consider alternative approaches for the estimation of a multilayer NAR, based on full Tucker decompositions.

Estimation of node factors

Once we compute the OLS estimator, let
\widehat{\nu}:=(\widehat{\nu}_{1}\cdots\widehat{\nu}_{T})^{\prime}
be the
T\times N
matrix of residuals of the FNAR such that

\widehat{\nu}_{t}:=y_{t}-\widehat{X}_{t}\widehat{\theta}^{\text{\tiny OLS}}
,
t=1,\ldots,T
. Then, the node factors
G_{t}
and their loadings
\Lambda
can be estimated by PCA in two equivalent ways.
Specifically, consider either the
N\times N
or
T\times T
sample covariance matrices

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\widehat{\nu}}:=\frac{\widehat{\nu}^{\prime}\widehat{\nu}}{T},\hskip 18.49988pt\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\widehat{\nu}}:=\frac{\widehat{\nu}\widehat{\nu}^{\prime}}{N}.

(.1)

Then, letting
\widehat{G}:=(\widehat{G}_{1},\cdots,\widehat{G}_{T})^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{G}:=(\widetilde{G}_{1},\cdots,\widetilde{G}_{T})^{\prime}
be the estimated
T\times q
matrices of factors, the PC estimators are given by:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\widehat{\Lambda}:=\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\nu}}(\widehat{M}^{\widehat{\nu}})^{1/2},\\
\widehat{G}:=\widehat{\nu}\widehat{\Lambda}(\widehat{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda})^{-1},\end{array}\right.\hskip 9.24994pt\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\widetilde{G}:=\widetilde{V}^{\widehat{\nu}}\sqrt{T},\\
\widetilde{\Lambda}:=\widehat{\nu}^{\prime}\widetilde{G}(\widetilde{G}^{\prime}\widetilde{G})^{-1},\end{array}\right.

where
\widehat{M}^{\widehat{\nu}}
is the
q\times q
diagonal matrix of eigenvalues of
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\widehat{\nu}}
with corresponding normalized eigenvectors being the columns of the
N\times q
matrix
\widehat{V}^{\widehat{\nu}}
, and
\widetilde{V}^{\widehat{\nu}}
is the
T\times q
matrix of normalized eigenvectors of
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\widehat{\nu}}
. It is easy to verify that, regardless of the choice made in (
Estimation of node factors
),
\widehat{G}\widehat{\Lambda}^{\prime}=\widetilde{G}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}
.

Notice that the estimated loadings and factors are such that
\widehat{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}/N
and
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widetilde{\Lambda}/N
are diagonal and

\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{G}_{t}\widehat{G}_{t}^{\prime}=I_{q},\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{G}_{t}\widetilde{G}_{t}^{\prime}=I_{q}

so that the estimated factors are orthonormal.

OLS estimator and its asymptotic properties

The OLS estimator of
\theta
is given by:

\widehat{\theta}^{\text{\tiny OLS}}:=\left(\sideset{}{{}_{t=1}^{T}}{\sum}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)^{-1}\left(\sideset{}{{}_{t=1}^{T}}{\sum}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}y_{t}\right).

(.1)

This is the estimator proposed by
Zhu et al. 2017
and
Chen et al. 2023
for the case in which the network is observed and, respectively, when the FNAR errors are either uncorrelated or have a factor structure with serially uncorrelated node factors.

We start by making the following standard assumption.

Assumption 12

(CLTs for FNAR - OLS)
.

(i)

For all
t,s\in\mathbb{Z}
with
t\neq s
,
\mathbb{E}[G_{t}G_{s}^{\prime}]=0
.

(ii)

As
N,T\to\infty
,

\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}^{\prime}\nu_{t}\overset{d}{\to}N(0_{r+2},\Omega_{0})
,
where

\Omega_{0}:=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{\prime}VX_{t}\right]
is an
(r+2)\times(r+2)
positive definite matrix.

(iii)

As
N,T\to\infty
,

\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}^{\prime}X_{t}\overset{p}{\to}\Sigma_{XX}
,
where

\Sigma_{XX}:=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}[X_{t}^{\prime}X_{t}]
is an
(r+2)\times(r+2)
positive definite matrix.

Because of Assumptions
12
(i)
,
6
(vii)
, and
7
, we have that the FNAR errors
\{\nu_{t}\}
are not autocorrelated. This is necessary for the CLT in the next part of this assumption to hold. Assumption
12
(ii)
is also found in
Bai and Ng 2006
. In fact, Assumptions
12
(ii)
and
12
(iii)
are made for simplicity and could be proved in a similar way as in
Chen et al. 2023
.

The OLS estimator in (
.1
) satisfies:

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}^{\text{\tiny OLS}}-\bar{J}\theta=

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sideset{}{{}_{t=1}^{T}}{\sum}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)^{-1}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sideset{}{{}_{t=1}^{T}}{\sum}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\nu_{t}\right)+\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sideset{}{{}_{t=1}^{T}}{\sum}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}u_{t}\right)\right\}.

(.2)

The asymptotic properties of the terms of (
.2
) are given in the following Proposition.

Proposition .1
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
10
and
12
, as
m,N,T\to\infty
,

(i)

\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}u_{t}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}T},\frac{1}{m},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}}\right)\right)
.

(ii)

\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\nu_{t}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{N^{2}T},\frac{1}{m},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}}\right)\right)
.

(iii)

\norm{\frac 1{NT}\sum_{t=1}^T \widehat{X}_{t}'\widehat X_{t}-\frac 1{NT}\sum_{t=1}^T\bar J {X}_{t}' X_{t}\bar J}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}T},\frac{1}{m},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}}\right)\right)
.

The next theorem follows.

Theorem .1

(CLT for FNAR coefficients estimated by OLS)
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
10
and
12
,
if
{\sqrt{T}}/m\to 0
, as
m,N,T\to\infty
,

\sqrt{T}(\widehat{\theta}^{\text{\tiny OLS}}-\bar{J}\theta)\overset{d}{\to}\mathcal{N}(0_{r+2},\Sigma_{XX}^{-1}\Omega_{0}\Sigma_{XX}^{-1}),

where
\Omega_{0}
and
\Sigma_{XX}
are defined in Assumptions
12
(ii)
and
12
(iii)
, respectively, and

\bar{J}
is defined in (
5.1
).

From Proposition
.1
, we see that if
\sqrt{T}/m\to 0
, as
m,N,T\to\infty
, then the network factors can be treated as observed and Theorem
.1
follows. In particular, by virtue of Assumptions
12
(ii)
and
12
(iii)
, the OLS estimator is
\sqrt{T}
-consistent and asymptotically normal. Notice that
the requirement
\sqrt{T}/m\to 0
for Theorem
.1
to hold is analogous to the one assumed in the vector case by
Bai and Ng 2006
.

An estimator of the asymptotic variance-covariance matrix of the OLS estimator is then given by (see
Bai and Ng 2006
, Theorem 1
):

\widehat{\Avar}\left[\sqrt{T}(\widehat{\theta}^{\text{\tiny OLS}}-\bar{J}\theta)\right]=\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{^{\prime}}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{^{\prime}}\widehat{\nu}_{t}\widehat{\nu}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{^{\prime}}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)^{-1}.

GLS estimator and its asymptotic properties

A GLS extension of the OLS estimator of the FNAR considered in Appendix
OLS estimator and its asymptotic properties
can be computed by means of the following procedure, initially proposed by
Chen et al. 2023
for the special case where the network is observed.

Once we estimate the node factors and their loadings as described in Appendix
Estimation of node factors
, let
\widehat{\epsilon}:=\widehat{\nu}-\widehat{G}\widehat{\Lambda}^{\prime}=\widehat{\nu}-\widetilde{G}\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}
and
\widehat{S}
be the diagonal matrix with entries the diagonal entries of
T^{-1}\widehat{\epsilon}^{\prime}\widehat{\epsilon}
. We can estimate the covariance matrix
V
of
\nu_{t}
as
\widehat{V}:=\widehat{\Lambda}\widehat{\Lambda}^{\prime}+\widehat{S}
and, by applying the Sherman-Morrison-Woodbury formula, its inverse as:

\widehat{V}^{-1}:=\widehat{S}^{-1}-\widehat{S}^{-1}\widehat{\Lambda}(I_{q}+\widehat{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{S}^{-1}\widehat{\Lambda})^{-1}\widehat{\Lambda}^{\prime}\widehat{S}^{-1},

(.1)

where
\widehat{S}^{-1}
is a diagonal matrix and hence easy to compute.

The GLS estimator of
\theta
is then given by:

\widehat{\theta}^{\text{\tiny GLS}}:=\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{V}^{-1}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)^{-1}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{V}^{-1}y_{t}\right).

(.2)

To study the asymptotic properties of the GLS estimator, we make two more assumptions. First, we extend Assumptions
12
(ii)
and
12
(iii)
to the following.

Assumption 13

(CLTs for FNAR - GLS)
.

(i)

For all
t,s\in\mathbb{Z}
with
t\neq s
,
\mathbb{E}[G_{t}G_{s}^{\prime}]=0
.

(ii)

As
N,T\to\infty
,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}^{\prime}V^{-1}\nu_{t}\overset{d}{\to}N(0_{r+2},\Omega_{1})
, where

\Omega_{1}:=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{\prime}V^{-1}X_{t}\right]
is an
(r+2)\times(r+2)
positive definite matrix.

(iii)

As
N,T\to\infty
,

\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}^{\prime}V^{-1}X_{t}\overset{p}{\to}\Omega_{1}
,
where

\Omega_{1}
is defined in part
(ii)
.

Because of Assumptions
13
(i)
,
6
(vii)
, and
7
, we have that the FNAR errors
\{\nu_{t}\}
are not autocorrelated. This is necessary for the CLT in the next part of this assumption to hold.
Assumptions
13
(ii)
and
13
(iii)
follow directly from
12
(ii)
and
12
(iii)
since we know that
V^{-1}
is finite for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Notice, however, the different role of
N
in the definition of
\Omega_{0}
and
\Omega_{1}
, indeed, as
N\to\infty
we have
X_{t}^{\prime}VX_{t}=O_{p}(N^{2})
, but
X_{t}^{\prime}V^{-1}X_{t}=O_{p}(N)
, since
X_{t}=O_{p}(\sqrt{N})
,
V=O(N)
and
V^{-1}=O(1)
.

Second, to study the properties of the GLS estimator (
.2
) we need to prove consistency of the estimated inverse of the FNAR errors covariance
\widehat{V}^{-1}
defined in (
.1
). This is not an easy task, for at least three reasons: first, the FNAR errors are estimated and not observed; second, the matrix
V
is
N\times N
so it is a high-dimensional one; third, to study
\widehat{V}^{-1}
, we need uniform consistency over all
N^{2}
entries of the estimated covariance
\widehat{V}
. These difficulties are reduced if we assume that all considered random variables are sub-Gaussian, which is a classical assumption in high-dimensional statistics
(see, e.g.,
Vershynin 2018
, Chapter 2)
.

Assumption 14

(Sub-Gaussianity)
.

(i)

For all
i\in\mathbb{N}
, all
j=1,\ldots,r+2
, and all
t\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\text{P}(\absolutevalue{X_{ijt}-\mathbb E[X_{ijt}]}>s)\leq 2\exp(-s^2/c_1^2)

for some finite
c_{1}
independent of
i,j
, and
t
.

(ii)

For all
i,j,k\in\mathbb{N}
and all
t\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\text{P}(\absolutevalue{\mathcal E_{tijk}}>s)\leq 2\exp(-s^2/c_2^2)
for some finite
c_{2}
independent of
i,j,k
, and
t
.

(iii)

For all
i\in\mathbb{N}
and all
t\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\text{P}(\absolutevalue{\epsilon_{it}}>s)\leq 2\exp(-s^2/c_3^2)
for some finite
c_{3}
independent of
i
and
t
.

This approach is similar to the one adopted in a vector factor model context by
Fan et al. 2013
. Instead,
Chen et al. 2023
assume a set of moment conditions on the regressors matrix
X_{t}
, which amount to bounding up the 8th order cross-cumulants (in addition, they also make use of Hanson-Wright concentration inequalities which are based
on the assumption of sub-Gaussianity).

The GLS estimator in (
.2
) satisfies:

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}^{\text{\tiny GLS}}-\bar{J}\theta=

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{V}^{-1}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)^{-1}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{V}^{-1}\nu_{t}\right)+\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{V}^{-1}u_{t}\right)\right\}.

(.3)

The asymptotic properties of the terms of (
.3
) are then given in the following Proposition.

Proposition .1
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
10
,
13
, and
14
, if
\sqrt{T}/m\to 0
, as
m,N,T\to\infty
,

(i)

\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{V}^{-1}u_{t}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}T},\frac{1}{m},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}}\right)\right)
.

(ii)

\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{V}^{-1}\nu_{t}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}},\frac{1}{N^{2}T},\frac{1}{m},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}}\right)\right)
.

(iii)

\norm{\frac 1{NT}\sum_{t=1}^T \widehat{X}_{t}'\widehat V^{-1}\widehat X_{t}-\frac 1{NT}\sum_{t=1}^T\bar J {X}_{t}' V^{-1} X_{t}\bar J}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}},\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}},\frac{1}{m},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}}\right)\right)
.

The next theorem follows.

Theorem .1

(CLT for FNAR coefficients estimated by GLS)
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
10
,
13
, and
14
, if
{\sqrt{NT}}/m\to 0
and
N/m\to 0
, as
m,N,T\to\infty
,

\sqrt{NT}(\widehat{\theta}^{\text{\tiny GLS}}-\bar{J}\theta)\overset{d}{\to}\mathcal{N}(0_{r+2},\Omega_{1}^{-1}),

where
\Omega_{1}
is defined in Assumptions
13
(ii)
and

\bar{J}
is defined in (
5.1
).

For observed network factors, the GLS estimator has a faster rate of convergence than the OLS estimator and it is more efficient; see also
Chen et al. 2023
. The different rates depend on the different scaling needed for Assumptions
12
(ii)
and
13
(ii)
to hold. Indeed, on the one hand
\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{\prime}VX_{t}\right]=O(N^{2})
, while, on the other hand
\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}^{\prime}V^{-1}X_{t}\right]=O(N)
. This is because, by Assumption
6
,
\norm{V}=O(N)
but
\norm{V^{-1}}=O(1)
.

Now, from Proposition
.1
, we see that the conditions
{\sqrt{NT}}/m\to 0
and
N/m\to 0
allow us to treat the factors as observed. These conditions are stronger than in the OLS case.

An estimator of the asymptotic variance-covariance matrix of the GLS estimator is then given by:

\widehat{\Avar}\left[\sqrt{NT}(\widehat{\theta}^{\text{\tiny GLS}}-\bar{J}\theta)\right]=\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{^{\prime}}\widehat{V}^{-1}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)^{-1},

where
\widehat{V}^{-1}
is defined in (
.1
). Alternatively, to address possible residual cross-correlation of the node idiosyncratic components, we could use:

\widehat{\Avar}\left[\sqrt{NT}(\widehat{\theta}^{\text{\tiny GLS}}-\bar{J}\theta)\right]=\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{^{\prime}}\widehat{V}^{-1}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{^{\prime}}\widehat{V}^{-1}\widehat{\nu}_{t}\widehat{\nu}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{V}^{-1}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{^{\prime}}\widehat{V}^{-1}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)^{-1}.

Proofs of the main results

.1
General statements of Assumptions
2
,

3
,

5
,
and
10

All the following results in Appendices
Proofs of the main results
and
Auxiliary lemmata
are proved under a more general version of the assumptions in the main text. Namely, we replace Assumptions

2
(ii)
,

2
(iv)
,

2
(v)
,

3
,

5
(i)
,
and
10
with:

Assumption 2
(General statement).

(ii)

For all
N\in\mathbb{N}
, all
t,s\in\mathbb{Z}
, and all
i,j=1,\ldots N^{2}
,

{N^{-\gamma}}\sum_{h=1}^{N^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{N^{2}}\absolutevalue{
\mathbb{E} \left[ \mathcal{E}_{(3) t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3) s j k} \right] }\leq\rho_{\mathcal{E}}^{\absolutevalue{t-s}}M_{ij}

and, for all
N\in\mathbb{N}
, all
t,s\in\mathbb{Z}
, and all
i,j,k=1,\dots,N^{2}
,

{N^{-\gamma}}\sum_{h=1}^{N^{2}}\absolutevalue{
\mathbb{E} \left[ \mathcal{E}_{(3) t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3) s j k} \right] }\leq\rho_{\mathcal{E}}^{\absolutevalue{t-s}}M_{ij}

for some
\gamma\in[0,2]
and some finite
\rho_{\mathcal{E}}
and
M_{ij}
independent of
t,s,k
and
N
such that

0\leq\rho_{\mathcal{E}}<1
,
\sum_{i=1,i\neq j}^{m}M_{ij}\leq M_{\mathcal{E}}
and
\sum_{j=1,j\neq i}^{m}M_{ij}\leq M_{\mathcal{E}}
, for some finite
M_{\mathcal{E}}
independent of
i,j
and
m
.

(iv)

For all
m,T,N\in\mathbb{N}
and all
j=1,\ldots,N^{2}
and all
s=1,\ldots,T
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\absolutevalue{ \frac{1}{\sqrt{mT} N^{\gamma} } \sum_{i=1}^{m} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \sum_{h=1}^{N^2} \sum_{k=1}^{N^2}
\left\{
\mathcal{E}_{(3)t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t j k}
-
\mathbb{E} \left[ \mathcal{E}_{(3)t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t j k} \right]
\right\} }^{2}\right]\leq C_{\mathcal{E}}

and

\mathbb{E}\left[\absolutevalue{ \frac{1}{\sqrt{mT} N^{\gamma} } \sum_{i=1}^{m} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \sum_{h=1}^{N^2} \sum_{k=1}^{N^2}
\left\{
\mathcal{E}_{(3)t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3)s i k}
-
\mathbb{E} \left[ \mathcal{E}_{(3)t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3)s i k} \right]
\right\} }^{2}\right]\leq C_{\mathcal{E}}

for some finite
C_{\mathcal{E}}
independent of
j,s,m,T,N
and some
\gamma\in[0,2]
.

(v)

For all
m,T,N\in\mathbb{N}
and all
j=1,\ldots,N^{2}
and all
s=1,\ldots,T
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\absolutevalue{ \frac{1}{\sqrt{mT} N^{\gamma/2} } \sum_{i=1}^{m} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \sum_{h=1}^{N^2}
\left\{
\mathcal{E}_{(3)t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t j h}
-
\mathbb{E} \left[ \mathcal{E}_{(3)t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t j h} \right]
\right\} }^{2}\right]\leq C_{\mathcal{E}}

and

\mathbb{E}\left[\absolutevalue{ \frac{1}{\sqrt{mT} N^{\gamma/2} } \sum_{i=1}^{m} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \sum_{h=1}^{N^2}
\left\{
\mathcal{E}_{(3)t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3)s i h}
-
\mathbb{E} \left[ \mathcal{E}_{(3)t i h } \mathcal{E}_{(3)s i h} \right]
\right\} }^{2}\right]\leq C_{\mathcal{E}}

for some finite
C_{\mathcal{E}}
independent of
j,s,m,T,N
and some
\gamma\in[0,2]
.

Assumption 3
(General statement).

For all
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
, all
k=1,\ldots,r
, and all
t\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{F}_{(3)tkj}\mathcal{E}_{(3)tij}]=0
,
and, for all
m,N,T\in\mathbb{N}
and all
t=1,\ldots,T
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{mN^{\gamma}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\norm{ \frac 1{\sqrt T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t i\cdot}^\prime}_{F}^{2}\right]\leq C_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{E}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{N^{2\gamma}}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathcal{F}_{(3)s}\left\{\mathcal{E}_{(3)si\cdot}^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)ti\cdot}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{E}_{(3)si\cdot}^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)ti\cdot}]\right\}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right]\leq C_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{E}}^{\prime},

for some finite
C_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{E}}
and
C_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
independent of
t
,
m,N
, and
T
and some
\gamma\in[0,2]
.

Assumption 5
(General statement).

(i)

For any given
i=1,\ldots,m
,
as
N,T\to\infty
,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{\gamma}T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}\mathcal{E}_{(3)ti\cdot}^{\prime}\overset{d}{\to}\mathcal{N}(0_{r},\Phi_{i}),

where

\Phi_{i}:=\lim_{N,T\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{\gamma}T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}\mathcal{E}_{(3)ti\cdot}^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{\gamma}T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}\mathcal{E}_{(3)ti\cdot}^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}\right],

for some
\gamma\in[0,2]
.

Assumption 10
(General statement).

For all
m,N,T\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac 1{\sqrt{mT}N^{1+\gamma/2}}\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^m u_i \mathcal E_{(3)ti\cdot} (y_{t-1}\otimes X_t)}^{2}\right]\leq\mathfrak{K}_{1},

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac 1{\sqrt{mT}N^{1+\gamma/2}}\sum_{t=1}^T\sum_{i=1}^m u_i \mathcal E_{(3)ti\cdot} (y_{t-1}\otimes\nu_t)}^{2}\right]\leq\mathfrak{K}_{2},

for some finite
\mathfrak{K}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{K}_{2}
independent of
m,N
, and
T
and some
\gamma\in[0,2]
.

These assumptions all depend on a generic
\gamma\in[0,2]
, thus are more general than those stated in the main text, which correspond to the case
\gamma=2
. All Theorems and Propositions in the main text and in Appendices
OLS estimator and its asymptotic properties
and
GLS estimator and its asymptotic properties
are stated in the case
\gamma=2
, which is the least favorable one, meaning it is the case giving the slowest possible convergence rates.

.2
Proof of Proposition
5.1

Proof.

First, let
A_{t}:=N^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\beta_{j}F_{j,t-1}+\rho
and
notice that (
3.2
) can be rewritten as

y_{t}=\alpha+A_{t}y_{t-1}+\nu_{t}.

(.1)

Thus, for any given
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
letting
y_{-\infty}=0
, if there exists a causal solution it is given by:

\displaystyle y_{t}

\displaystyle=\left\{\prod_{k=0}^{\ell}(\alpha+A_{t-k})\right\}y_{t-\ell-1}+\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\left\{\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}(\alpha+A_{t-k})\right\}\nu_{t-j}+\nu_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\left\{\prod_{k=0}^{j-1}(\alpha+A_{t-k})\right\}\nu_{t-j}+\nu_{t}.

(.2)

To this end first notice that since by Assumption
7
,
\{\mathcal{F}_{t}\}
is independent of
\{\nu_{t}\}
, for any given
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\mathbb{E}[y_{t}]=\alpha+\rho\mathbb{E}[y_{t-1}]+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\beta_{j}\mathbb{E}[F_{j,t-1}]\mathbb{E}[y_{t-1}]

hence, a stationary solution must have mean

\mathbb{E}[y_{t}]=\left(I_{N}-\rho I_{N}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\beta_{j}\mathbb{E}[F_{j,t}]\right)^{-1}\alpha,

which is finite and independent of
t
because of Assumptions
1
(ii)
and
9
(i)
. Clearly if
\alpha=0
then
\mathbb{E}[y_{t}]=0
and vice versa.

Let then
\alpha=0
for simplicity and define
\Sigma_{t,s}:=\mathbb{E}[y_{t}y_{s}^{\prime}]
and recall that
V:=\mathbb{E}[\nu_{t}\nu_{t}^{\prime}]
, then

\displaystyle\text{vec}(\Sigma_{t,t})

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\text{vec}(\Sigma_{t-1,t-1})+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\beta_{j}^{2}\mathbb{E}[F_{j,t-1}\otimes F_{j,t-1}]\text{vec}(\Sigma_{t-1,t-1})+\text{vec}(V)

\displaystyle=\left\{\rho^{2}I_{N^{2}}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\beta_{j}^{2}\mathbb{E}[F_{j,t-1}\otimes F_{j,t-1}]\right\}\text{vec}(\Sigma_{t-1,t-1})+\text{vec}(V)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}\left\{\rho^{2}I_{N^{2}}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\beta_{j}^{2}\mathbb{E}[F_{j,t-1}\otimes F_{j,t-1}]\right\}^{k}\text{vec}(V)

\displaystyle+\left\{\rho^{2}I_{N^{2}}+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\beta_{j}^{2}\mathbb{E}[F_{j,t-1}\otimes F_{j,t-1}]\right\}^{\ell+1}\text{vec}(\Sigma_{t-\ell-1,t-\ell-1}).

(.3)

Notice also that
V
is positive definite, indeed its smallest eigenvalue is such that, by Weyl’s inequality,

\mu_{N}(V)\geq\mu_{N}(\Lambda\Lambda^{\prime})+\mu_{N}(S)=\min_{i=1,\ldots,N}\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{it}^{2}]\geq\underline{M}_{\epsilon},

because of Assumption
6
(vi)
and where
\mu_{N}(\Lambda\Lambda^{\prime})
,
\mu_{N}(V)
, and
\mu_{N}(S)
are the smallest eigenvalues of
\Lambda\Lambda^{\prime}
,
V
, and
S
, respectively, and
\mu_{N}(\Lambda\Lambda^{\prime})=0
.

Hence, letting
\ell\to\infty
we see that
\text{vec}(\Sigma_{t,t})
is finite and independent of
t
, because of Assumptions
1
(ii)
and
9
(ii)
, and since
V
is positive definite, see also Theorem 2.1 in
Nicholls and Quinn 1981
.

To show that the above arguments imply that also
y_{t}^{\omega}=\lim_{N\to\infty}\omega^{\prime}y_{t}
exists almost surely and is stationary and causal it is enough to follow the same steps as in Theorem 2 by
Zhu et al. 2017
.
∎

.3
Proof of Theorem
5.1

Proof.

The proof of part
(i)
follows directly from Lemmas
.11
(ii)
and
.13
.

For part
(ii)
, from (
.5
) in the proof of Lemma
.11
(i)
and Lemma
.13
(i)
, if
N^{\gamma/2}\sqrt{T}/m\to 0
, as
m,N,T\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle N^{2-\gamma/2}\sqrt{T}\left(\widehat{u}_{i}^{\prime}-u_{i}^{\prime}J\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{N^{2-\gamma/2}\sqrt{T}}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{E}_{(3)ti\cdot}\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}u_{j}u_{j}^{\prime}\right)\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}J+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{\gamma}T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{E}_{(3)ti\cdot}\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}^{\prime}\Sigma_{U}\left(\frac{{M}^{\chi}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}J+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{\gamma}T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{E}_{(3)ti\cdot}\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}^{\prime}J+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{d}{\to}\mathcal{N}(0_{r},J_{0}\Phi_{i}J_{0}),

because of Assumption
4
(i)
and Lemma
.6
(iii)
, Assumption
5
(i)
, and Slutsky’s Theorem, and where
J_{0}=\plim_{m,N,T\to\infty}J
. Notice that
J\Phi_{i}J=\Phi_{i}
. This proves part
(ii)
.
The final statement follows by setting
\gamma=2
.
∎

.4
Proof of Theorem
5.2

Proof.

For part
(i)
, consider the estimated factors
\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{(3)}
. We have that:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{W}_{t}\times_{3}\left(\widehat{U}^{\prime}\widehat{U}\right)^{-1}\widehat{U}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathcal{F}_{t}\times_{3}U+\mathcal{E}_{t}\right)\times_{3}\left(\widehat{U}^{\prime}\widehat{U}\right)^{-1}\widehat{U}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}_{t}\times_{3}\left(\widehat{U}^{\prime}\widehat{U}\right)^{-1}\widehat{U}^{\prime}\left(U-\widehat{U}J+\widehat{U}J\right)+\mathcal{E}_{t}\times_{3}\left(\widehat{U}^{\prime}\widehat{U}\right)^{-1}\left(\widehat{U}-\widehat{U}J+\widehat{U}J\right)^{\prime},

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{(3)t}

\displaystyle=\left(\widehat{U}^{\prime}\widehat{U}\right)^{-1}\left[\widehat{U}^{\prime}\left(U-\widehat{U}J+\widehat{U}J\right)\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}\right]+\left(\widehat{U}-UJ+UJ\right)^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}\left[\frac{\widehat{U}^{\prime}\left(U-\widehat{U}J\right)}{m}\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}+\frac{\widehat{U}^{\prime}\widehat{U}}{m}J\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}+\frac{\left(\widehat{U}-UJ\right)^{\prime}}{m}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}+\frac{JU^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{m}\right].

Thus, because of Lemma
.7
(iv)
,

\displaystyle\frac{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{(3)t}-J\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}}{N}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}\left[\frac{\widehat{U}^{\prime}\left(U-\widehat{U}J\right)}{mN}\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}+\frac{\left(\widehat{U}-UJ\right)^{\prime}}{mN}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}+\frac{JU^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{mN}\right]

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1)\left[A+B+C\right].

(.4)

For term (A), given (
.11
) in the proof of Lemma
.13
when
\widehat{H}=J
and Lemma
.4
(ii)

\norm{\frac{ \widehat{U}' \left(U - \widehat{U} J \right) }{mN} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t}}\leq\norm{ \frac{ \widehat{U}' \left(U - \widehat{U} J \right) }{m} }\ \norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}}{N}}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\xi}\right),

(.5)

where
\xi=\min\left(\sqrt{mT}N^{2-\gamma/2},N^{3-\gamma/2}T,m\sqrt{T}N^{2-\gamma},mN^{2-\gamma}\right)
.
For term (B), from (
.4
) in the proof of Lemma
.11
(i)
with
\widehat{H}=J
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\widehat{U}-UJ\right)^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{mN}=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}J\frac{U^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)}\mathcal{F}_{(3)}^{\prime}U^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{m^{2}N^{3}T}+\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}J\frac{U^{\prime}U\mathcal{F}_{(3)}\mathcal{E}_{(3)}^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{m^{2}N^{3}T}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}J\frac{U^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)}\mathcal{E}_{(3)}^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{m^{2}N^{3}T}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}(\widehat{U}-UJ)^{\prime}\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)}\mathcal{F}_{(3)}^{\prime}U^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{m^{2}N^{3}T}+\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}(\widehat{U}-UJ)^{\prime}\frac{U\mathcal{F}_{(3)}\mathcal{E}_{(3)}^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{m^{2}N^{3}T}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}(\widehat{U}-UJ)^{\prime}\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)}\mathcal{E}_{(3)}^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{m^{2}N^{3}T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle III_{a}+III_{b}+III_{c}+III_{d}+III_{e}+III_{f}.

Then, because of Lemma
.4
(i)
,
.4
(iii)
, and
.7
(iv)
, and using (
.10
) and
\norm{J}=O(1)
,

\displaystyle\norm{III_a}

\displaystyle\leq\norm{\left( \frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^2} \right)^{-1}}\ \norm{J}\ \norm{ \frac{U' \mathcal{E}_{(3)} \mathcal{F}_{(3)}'}{m N^2 T} }\ \norm{\frac{U}{\sqrt{m}}}\ \norm{\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{\sqrt{m}N}}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1)O(1)O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{2-\gamma/2}}\right)O_{p}(1)O\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{3-\gamma}}\right).

Turning to
III_{b}
we have

\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)}\mathcal{E}_{(3)}' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t} }{m N^3 T}}\leq\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)}\left\{\mathcal{E}_{(3)}' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3)}' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}\right]\right\}}{mN^3 T}}+\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3)}' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}\right] }{m N^3 T}}.

(.6)

By Assumption
3
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)}\left\{\mathcal{E}_{(3)}' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3)}' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}\right]\right\}}{mN^3 T}}^{2}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)}\left\{\mathcal{E}_{(3)}' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3)}' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}\right]\right\}}{mN^3 T}}^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m^{2}N^{6}T^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathcal{F}_{(3)s}\left\{\mathcal{E}_{(3)si\cdot}^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)ti\cdot}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{E}_{(3)si\cdot}^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)ti\cdot}]\right\}\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{C_{\mathcal{F}\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}{mN^{6-2\gamma}T}.

(.7)

Moreover, by Assumption
1
(vi)
and Lemma
.1
(ii)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3)}' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}\right] }{m N^3 T}}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3)}' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}\right] }{m N^3 T}}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{mN^{3}T}\mathbb{E}\left[\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sum_{k=1}^{N^{2}}\left(\sum_{s=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{F}_{(3)slh}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3)sih}\mathcal{E}_{(3)tik}\right]\right)^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{mN^{3}T}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sum_{k=1}^{N^{2}}\left|\sum_{s=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{F}_{(3)slh}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3)sih}\mathcal{E}_{(3)tik}\right]\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{mN^{3}T}\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sum_{k=1}^{N^{2}}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\mathcal{F}_{(3)slh}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3)sih}\mathcal{E}_{(3)tik}\right]\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{mN^{3}T}\max_{l=1,\ldots,r}\max_{s=1,\ldots,T}\max_{h=1,\ldots,N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\mathcal{F}_{(3)slh}\right|\right]\sum_{k=1}^{N^{2}}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\sum_{h=1}^{N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3)sih}\mathcal{E}_{(3)tik}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{rC_{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}N^{\gamma}m}{mN^{3}T}.

(.8)

From (
.6
), (
.7
), and (
.8
),

\displaystyle\norm{III_b}

\displaystyle\leq\norm{\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^2}\right)^{-1}}\ \norm{J}\ \norm{\frac{U'U }{m}}\ \norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)}\mathcal{E}_{(3)}' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t} }{m N^3 T}}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{3-\gamma}}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{TN^{3-\gamma}}\right).

Because of Lemma
.4
(i)
,
.4
(iii)
, and Lemma
.10
(ii)
,
.7
(iv), and since
\norm{J}=O(1)
,

\displaystyle\norm{III_c}

\displaystyle\leq\norm{\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^2}\right)^{-1}}\ \norm{J}\ \norm{\frac{U \mathcal{E}_{(3)} \mathcal{E}_{(3)}' }{m^{3/2} N^2T}}\norm{\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)t} }{\sqrt{m} N}}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{3-\gamma}},\frac{1}{mN^{3(1-\gamma/2)}}\right)\right).

Next, notice that, by Assumption
1
(i)

and Lemma
.1
(iii)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{U' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{mN}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{U' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{mN}}^{2}_{F}\right]=\frac{1}{m^{2}N^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{u_k' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m^{2}N^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{h=1}^{N^{2}}U_{ik}\mathcal{E}_{(3)tih}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r}{m^{2}N^{2}}\max_{k=1,\dots,r}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{h=1}^{N^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{N^{2}}\absolutevalue{ U_{ik} }\absolutevalue{ U_{jk} }\absolutevalue{\mathbb{E}\left[ \mathcal{E}_{(3)t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t j l} \right]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{rM_{U}^{2}}{m^{2}N^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{h=1}^{N^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{N^{2}}\absolutevalue{\mathbb{E}\left[ \mathcal{E}_{(3)t i h}
\mathcal{E}_{(3)t j l} \right]}\leq\frac{rM_{U}^{2}M_{\mathcal{E}}}{mN^{2-\gamma}}

(.9)

since
M_{\mathcal{E}}
does not depend on
t
.
Therefore, by Theorem
5.1
(i)
, Lemma
.10
(i)
,
.7
(iv)
, and
using (
.9
)
and
\norm{J}=O(1)

\displaystyle\norm{III_d}

\displaystyle\leq\norm{\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^2}\right)^{-1}}\ \norm{\frac{\widehat{U} - UJ}{\sqrt{m}}}\ \norm{\frac{ \mathcal{E}_{(3)} \mathcal{F}_{(3)}'}{\sqrt{m}N^2T}}\norm{\frac{ U' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t} }{m N }}

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1)O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{2-\gamma/2}\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{N^{2-\gamma}m}\right)\right)O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}N^{2-\gamma/2}}\right)O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{5-3\gamma/2}T\sqrt{m}},\frac{1}{N^{5-2\gamma}m^{3/2}\sqrt{T}}\right)\right).

Note that the term
III_{d}
is clearly dominated by
III_{a}
. Analogously,
III_{e}
and
III_{f}
are dominated by
III_{b}
and
III_{c}
, respectively. Thus, (B) is
O_{p}(1/(\sqrt{mT}N^{3-\gamma},mN^{3-3\gamma/2},TN^{3-\gamma}))
, hence it is dominated by term (A).

For term (C), using (
.9
)

\norm{\frac{ J U' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t} }{mN}}\leq\norm{ J}\norm{\frac{U' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{mN}}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right).

(.10)

By noticing that

\max\left(\frac{1}{\xi},\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)=\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right),

we prove part
(i)
.

For part
(ii)
, for any given
j=1,\ldots,N^{2}
, following the same reasoning as in part (i), we have

{\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{(3)t\cdot j}-J\mathcal{F}_{(3)t\cdot j}}=N\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}J\left[\frac{U^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t\cdot j}}{mN}+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N^{3}T}\right)\right].

Moreover,

\norm{\frac{U' \mathcal{E}_{(3)t\cdot j}}{m}}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\right).

Therefore, if
\sqrt{m}/(N^{2}T)\to 0
as
m,N,T\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{m}\left({\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{(3)t\cdot j}-J\mathcal{F}_{(3)t\cdot j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}J\left[\frac{U^{\prime}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t\cdot j}}{\sqrt{m}}\right]+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{U}^{-1}J\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}u_{i}\mathcal{E}_{(3)tij}\right]+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{d}{\to}\mathcal{N}\left(0_{r},\Sigma_{U}^{-1}J_{0}\Pi_{tj}J_{0}\Sigma_{U}^{-1}\right),

because of Assumption
4
(i)
, Lemma
.6
(iii)
,
Assumption
5
(ii)
, and Slutsky’s theorem, and
J_{0}=\plim_{m,N,T\to\infty}J
. Notice that
\Sigma_{U}^{-1}J_{0}\Pi_{tj}J_{0}\Sigma_{U}^{-1}=\Sigma_{U}^{-1}\Pi_{tj}\Sigma_{U}^{-1}
. This proves part
(ii)
.
∎

.5
Proof of Theorem
5.3

Proof.

First, notice that

\norm{M_{\widehat G^\dag}}=\norm{I_T-\widehat G^\dag\widehat G^{\dag'}/T}=O(1),\hskip 18.49988pt\norm{M_{\widehat\Lambda^*}}=\norm{I_N-\widehat V^{\widehat\nu^*}\widehat V^{\widehat{\nu}^{*'}} }=O(1)

(.11)

since
\norm{\widehat G^\dag}=O_{p}(\sqrt{T})
and eigenvectors are normalized.
Then, because of (
.11
) and by the same arguments used in the proof of Proposition
.1
(i)
, we have

\displaystyle\norm{\frac 1{NT}\sum_{i=1}^N\widehat{X}_i'M_{\widehat G^\dag} u_i }=

\displaystyle\,O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)\right),

(.12)

\displaystyle\norm{\frac 1{NT}\sum_{t=1}^T\widehat{X}_t'M_{\widehat\Lambda^*} u_t }=

\displaystyle\,O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)\right).

(.13)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\norm{\frac 1{NT}\sum_{i=1}^N \widehat{X}_{i}' M_{\widehat G^\dag} \widehat X_{i}-\frac 1{NT} \sum_{i=1}^N \bar J \widehat{X}_{i}' M_{\widehat G^\dag} \widehat X_{i}\bar J}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)\right),

(.14)

\displaystyle\norm{\frac 1{NT}\sum_{t=1}^T \widehat{X}_{t}' M_{\widehat\Lambda^*} \widehat X_{t}-\frac 1{NT} \sum_{t=1}^T \bar J \widehat{X}_{t}' M_{\widehat\Lambda^*} \widehat X_{t}\bar J}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)\right),

(.15)

because of (
.11
) and following Proposition
.1
(iii)
.
And also,

\displaystyle{\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{X}_{i}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{G}^{\dagger}}\,\left[G\Lambda^{\prime}+\epsilon\right]}=

\displaystyle\,{\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}X_{i}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{G}^{\dagger}}\left\{G\Lambda_{i}+\epsilon_{i}\right\}}

\displaystyle+{\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(\widehat{X}_{i}^{\prime}-\bar{J}X_{i}^{\prime}\right)M_{\widehat{G}^{\dagger}}\left\{G\Lambda_{i}+\epsilon_{i}\right\}}=:A+B,

(.16)

\displaystyle{\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}\,\left[\Lambda G_{t}+\epsilon_{t}\right]}=

\displaystyle\,{\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}\left\{\Lambda G_{t}+\epsilon_{t}\right\}}

\displaystyle+{\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}-\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}\right)M_{\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}\left\{\Lambda G_{t}+\epsilon_{t}\right\}}=:C+D,.

(.17)

Then, because of (
.12
), (
.13
), (
.14
), and (
.15
)

\displaystyle\norm{B}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)\right),

(.18)

\displaystyle\norm{D}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)\right).

(.19)

Consider first part
(i)
. If
{\sqrt{NT}}/(mN^{2-\gamma})\to 0
and
{\sqrt{N}}/(\sqrt{m}N^{1-\gamma/2})\to 0
, as
m,N,T\to\infty
,
by substituting (
.18
) into (
.16
), from (
5.2
) we get:

\displaystyle\sqrt{NT}\left(\widehat{\theta}^{\dagger}-\bar{J}\theta\right)=

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}{X}_{i}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{G}^{\dagger}}X_{i}\bar{J}\right)^{-1}\left(\sqrt{NT}\,A\right)+o_{p}(1)=:I+o_{p}(1).

(.20)

By similar arguments to those used in (
.14
),

\displaystyle\norm{\frac 1{NT} \sum_{i=1}^N \left(y_i-\widehat X_i\widehat\theta^{\dag}\right) \left(y_i-\widehat X_i\widehat\theta^{\dag}\right)'-
\frac 1{NT} \sum_{i=1}^N \left(y_i- X_i\bar J\widehat\theta^{\dag}\right) \left(y_i- X_i\bar J\widehat\theta^{\dag}\right)'}

\displaystyle\phantom{cippirimerlo}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)\right).

(.21)

Thus, from (
.21
) and the definition of
\widehat{G}^{\dagger}
in (
4.10
) it is clear that, if
{\sqrt{NT}}/(mN^{2-\gamma})\to 0
and
{\sqrt{N}}/(\sqrt{m}N^{1-\gamma/2})\to 0
, as
m,N,T\to\infty
, it solves

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(y_{i}-X_{i}\bar{J}\widehat{\theta}^{{\dagger}}\right)\left(y_{i}-X_{i}\bar{J}\widehat{\theta}^{{\dagger}}\right)^{\prime}+o_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)\right\}\widehat{G}^{\dagger}=\widehat{G}^{\dagger}\frac{\widehat{M}^{\widehat{\nu}^{\dagger}}}{T},

(.22)

where
\widehat{M}^{\widehat{\nu}^{\dagger}}
is the
q\times q
diagonal matrix of eigenvalues of

.

Now, define the
T\times(r+2)
matrices:

\displaystyle\widehat{Z}_{i}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle:=\left\{M_{\widehat{G}^{\dagger}}X_{i}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{k}\right)M_{\widehat{G}^{\dagger}}X_{k}\right\},

\displaystyle Z_{i}

\displaystyle:=\left\{M_{G}X_{i}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{k}\right)M_{G}X_{k}\right\}.

Then, following the same steps as in
Bai 2009
, if
{\sqrt{NT}}/(mN^{2-\gamma})\to 0
and
{\sqrt{N}}/(\sqrt{m}N^{1-\gamma/2})\to 0
, as

, we have that

in (
.20
) is such that

\displaystyle I=

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}\widehat{Z}_{i}^{{\dagger}^{\prime}}\widehat{Z}_{i}^{\dagger}\bar{J}\right)^{-1}\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}\widehat{Z}_{i}^{\dagger}\epsilon_{i}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sqrt{\frac{N}{T}}\left[\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}X_{i}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{G}^{\dagger}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{k}\epsilon_{k}^{\prime}]\right)\widehat{G}^{\dagger}\left(\frac{G^{\prime}\widehat{G}^{\dagger}}{T}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{i}\right]\right\}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}\widehat{Z}_{i}^{{\dagger}^{\prime}}\widehat{Z}_{i}^{{\dagger}}\bar{J}\right)^{-1}\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}Z_{i}\epsilon_{i}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sqrt{\frac{N}{T}}\left[\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}X_{i}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{G}^{\dagger}}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{k}\epsilon_{k}^{\prime}]\right)\widehat{G}^{\dagger}\left(\frac{G^{\prime}\widehat{G}^{\dagger}}{T}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{i}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sqrt{\frac{T}{N}}\left[\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}\left(X_{i}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{k}\right)X_{k}\right)^{\prime}G\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\Lambda_{j}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\epsilon_{jt}\epsilon_{it}\right)\right]\right\}+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}Z_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i}\bar{J}\right)^{-1}\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}Z_{i}\epsilon_{i}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sqrt{\frac{N}{T}}\left[\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}X_{i}^{\prime}M_{G}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{k}\epsilon_{k}^{\prime}]\right)G\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{i}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sqrt{\frac{T}{N}}\left[\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}\left(X_{i}-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{k}\right)X_{k}\right)^{\prime}G\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\Lambda_{j}\left(\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{jt}\epsilon_{it}]\right)\right]\right\}

\displaystyle+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{N}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{N}}{T}\right)+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}Z_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i}\bar{J}\right)^{-1}\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}Z_{i}\epsilon_{i}+\sqrt{\frac{N}{T}}I_{a}+\sqrt{\frac{T}{N}}I_{b}\right\}

\displaystyle+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{N}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{N}}{T}\right)+o_{p}(1).

(.23)

Since by Assumptions
6

and
6

,
\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{k}\epsilon_{k}^{\prime}]=\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{kt}^{2}]I_{T}=\sigma^{2}_{k}I_{T}
and since

, we have

. Moreover, since we assumed

and

, as

, by using (

) into (

we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{NT}\left(\widehat{\theta}^{\dagger}-\bar{J}\theta\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}Z_{i}^{\prime}Z_{i}\bar{J}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}Z_{i}\epsilon_{i}\right)+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{p}{\to}\mathcal{N}\left(0_{r+2},\bar{J}_{0}\Sigma_{ZZ}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}D_{1}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}\Sigma_{ZZ}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}\right),

, and Slutsky’s theorem and where

. We complete the proof by noticing that
\bar{J}_{0}\Sigma_{ZZ}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}D_{1}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}\Sigma_{ZZ}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}:=\Sigma_{ZZ}^{-1}D_{1}\Sigma_{ZZ}^{-1}
.

For part

, notice that if
\sigma_{i}^{2}=\sigma^{2}
for all

, then in (
.23
) we have

\displaystyle I_{b}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}X_{i}^{\prime}G\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{i}\sigma^{2}-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\left(\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{k}\right)X_{k}^{\prime}G\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{i}\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}X_{i}^{\prime}G\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{i}\sigma^{2}-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\bar{J}X_{k}^{\prime}G\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Lambda_{i}\Lambda_{i}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{k}\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bar{J}X_{i}^{\prime}G\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{i}\sigma^{2}-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\bar{J}X_{k}^{\prime}G\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\Lambda}{N}\right)^{-1}\Lambda_{k}\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,0_{r+2}.

The proof then follows as in part
(i)
and by noticing that in this case
\bar{J}_{0}\Sigma_{ZZ}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}D_{1}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}\Sigma_{ZZ}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}=\sigma^{2}\Sigma_{ZZ}^{-1}
.

For part
(iii)
. By similar arguments to those used in (
.14
),

\displaystyle\norm{\frac 1{NT} \sum_{t=1}^T \left(y_t-\widehat X_t\widehat\theta^{*}\right) \left(y_t-\widehat X_t\widehat\theta^{*}\right)'-
\frac 1{NT} \sum_{t=1}^T \left(y_t- X_t\bar J\widehat\theta^{*}\right) \left(y_t- X_t\bar J\widehat\theta^{*}\right)'}

\displaystyle\phantom{cippirimerlo}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)\right).

Thus, from (

) and the definition of
\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}
in (

) it is clear that, if
{\sqrt{NT}}/(mN^{2-\gamma})\to 0
and

, from (

\displaystyle\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}\frac{\widehat{M}^{\widehat{\nu}^{*}}}{N}=

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}\bar{J}(\bar{J}\theta-\widehat{\theta}^{*})\epsilon_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda G_{t}(\bar{J}\theta-\widehat{\theta}^{*})^{\prime}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\epsilon_{t}(\bar{J}\theta-\widehat{\theta}^{*})^{\prime}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda G_{t}\epsilon_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\epsilon_{t}G_{t}^{\prime}\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\epsilon_{t}\epsilon_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Lambda G_{t}G_{t}^{\prime}\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\,I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}+I_{5}+I_{6}+I_{7}+I_{8}+\Lambda\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{N}+o_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).

since
T^{-1}G^{\prime}G=I_{q}
by Assumption
8
(ii)
. So,

\displaystyle\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}\frac{\widehat{M}^{\widehat{\nu}^{*}}}{N}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{N}\right)^{-1}-\Lambda=\sum_{j=1}^{8}I_{j}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{N}\right)^{-1}+o_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{N}\right)^{-1}\right\|=O_{p}(1),

(.29)

Now,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\left\|I_{1}\right\|\leq\left\|\frac{\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{\sqrt{N}}\right\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\|X_{t}\|^{2}}{N}\left\|\widehat{\theta}^{*}-\bar{J}\theta\right\|^{2}=o_{p}\left(\|\widehat{\theta}^{*}-\bar{J}\theta\|\right),

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T^{2}N^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\|X_{t}\|^{2}\|X_{s}\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\|X_{t}\|^{4}\right]=\frac{1}{N^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}X_{it}^{2}\right)^{2}\right]

From (

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}\left\{I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}+I_{5}+I_{6}+I_{7}+I_{8}\right\}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{N}\right)^{-1}G_{t}

\displaystyle+o_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)+o_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\,J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}+J_{4}+J_{5}+J_{6}+J_{7}+J_{8}+O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}},\frac{1}{T}\right)\right).

Then, for

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}I_{3}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{N}\right)^{-1}G_{t}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)\left\{O_{p}(\widehat{\theta}^{*}-\bar{J}\theta)+O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)\right)\right\}.

For
J_{7}
we have

\displaystyle J_{7}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}I_{7}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{N}\right)^{-1}G_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\epsilon_{s}G_{s}^{\prime}\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{N}\right]\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{N}\right)^{-1}G_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{NT^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}G_{s}^{\prime}G_{t}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}\epsilon_{s}.

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}I_{8}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{N}\right)^{-1}G_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}\left[\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left\{\epsilon_{s}\epsilon_{s}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{s}\epsilon_{s}^{\prime}]+\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{s}\epsilon_{s}^{\prime}]\right\}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}\right]\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{N}\right)^{-1}G_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{N^{2}T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{s}\epsilon_{s}^{\prime}]\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{N}\right)^{-1}G_{t}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}M_{\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}\left[\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\left\{\epsilon_{s}\epsilon_{s}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{s}\epsilon_{s}^{\prime}]\right\}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}\right]\left(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}\widehat{\Lambda}^{*}}{N}\right)^{-1}G_{t}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right)O_{p}(\widehat{\theta}^{*}-\bar{J}\theta).

.6
Proof of Proposition
.1

Proof.

First, consider part
(i)
.
We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}u_{t}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}u_{t}+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}-\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}\right)u_{t}=a+b.

(.52)

Then, notice that we can write

u_{t}=(X_{t}\bar{J}-\widehat{X}_{t})\bar{J}\theta=\text{mat}_{1}\left(N^{-1}(\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\times_{3}J-\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1})\times_{2}y^{\prime}_{t-1}\times_{3}\beta^{\prime}J\right).

For term
a
, we have that

\displaystyle\norm{a}

\displaystyle=\norm{ \bar J X_t' \text{mat}_1 \left( \frac{1}{N^2T} \sum_{t=1}^T \left(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1} - \mathcal{F}_{t-1} \times_3 J \right) \times_2 y_{t-1}' \times_3 \beta' J \right)}

\displaystyle=\norm{ \text{mat}_1 \left( \frac{1}{N^2T} \sum_{t=1}^T \left(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1} - \mathcal{F}_{t-1} \times_3 J \right) \times_1 \bar J X_t' \times_2 y_{t-1}' \times_3 \beta' J \right)}

\displaystyle=\norm{ \text{mat}_3 \left( \frac{1}{N^2T} \sum_{t=1}^T \left(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1} - \mathcal{F}_{t-1} \times_3 J \right) \times_1 \bar J X_t' \times_2 y_{t-1}' \times_3 \beta' J \right)^\prime}

\displaystyle=\norm{ \beta' J \text{mat}_3 \left( \frac{1}{N^2T} \sum_{t=1}^T \left(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1} - \mathcal{F}_{t-1} \times_3 J \right) \times_1 \bar J X_t' \times_2 y_{t-1}' \right)}

\displaystyle\leq\norm{\beta J}\cdot\norm{\text{mat}_3 \left( \frac{1}{N^2T} \sum_{t=1}^T \left(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1} - \mathcal{F}_{t-1} \times_3 J \right) \times_1 \bar J X_t' \times_2 y_{t-1}' \right)}

\displaystyle=\norm{a_1}\cdot\norm{a_2}.

(.53)

Clearly,
\norm{a_1}=O(1)
.
As for

, let us first define

Then, recall that for a generic tensor

.4

\displaystyle a_{2}=

\displaystyle\text{mat}_{3}\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}-\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\times_{3}J\right)\times_{1}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}\times_{2}y_{t-1}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\text{mat}_{3}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}-\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\times_{3}J\right)\left(y_{t-1}^{\prime}\otimes\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}\right)^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{(3)t-1}-J\mathcal{F}_{(3)t-1}\right)\left(y_{t-1}\otimes X_{t}\bar{J}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_{(3)t-1}-J\mathcal{F}_{(3)t-1}\right)z_{t}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\widehat{U}^{\prime}\left(U-\widehat{U}J\right)}{m}\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}z_{t}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\left(\widehat{U}-UJ\right)^{\prime}}{m}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}z_{t}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{J^{\prime}U^{\prime}}{m}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}z_{t}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}\right)^{-1}\left\{a_{I}+a_{II}+a_{III}\right\}.

Now, because of (

) in the proof of Lemma

when

\displaystyle=\norm{\frac{1}{N^2T} \sum_{t=1}^T \frac{ \widehat{U}' \left(U - \widehat{U}J \right) }{m} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} z_t}_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\norm{\frac{ \widehat{U}' \left(U - \widehat{U}J \right) }{m} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t}}_{F}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\norm{z_t}_{F}^{2}\right)^{1/2}

and since

because of Assumption
1
(ii)
, and

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{N^{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{r+2}\mathbb{E}\left[z_{ijt}^{2}\right]\leq\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i,j=1}^{N}\sum_{k=1}^{r+2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[y_{it}^{4}\right]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[X_{jkt}^{4}\right]}
