b_{2}
is given by

\frac{1}{mN^{2}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{m}u_{i}\mathcal{E}_{(3)ti\cdot}\left(y_{t-1}\otimes\left\{y_{t-1}\otimes I_{N}\right\}^{\prime}\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)t-1}^{\prime}U}{mN}\right),

(.59)

which is dominated by term
a_{III}
because of (
.10
) in the proof of Theorem
5.2
(i)
. Therefore,
b_{2}
is dominated by
a_{2}
.
By combining (
.52
), (
.53
), and (
.57
), we complete the proof of part
(i)
.

Next, consider part
(ii)
.
We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{TN}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\nu_{t}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{TN}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}\nu_{t}+\frac{1}{TN}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}-\bar{J}X^{\prime}_{t}\right)\nu_{t}=A+B.

(.60)

By Assumption
12
(ii)
,

\norm{A}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right).

As for term
B
, we have

\displaystyle\norm{B}=\norm{\frac{1}{NT} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \left(\widehat X_{t}' - \bar J X'_t \right) \nu_t }

\displaystyle=\norm{ \frac{1}{N^2T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \left( \text{mat}_1 ( (\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_t -\mathcal{F}_t \times_3 J ) \times_2 y_{t-1}' ), 0_{N \times N}, 0_{N \times N} \right)' \nu_t}

\displaystyle=\norm{ \frac{1}{N^2T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \left( \text{mat}_1 \left( (\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_t -\mathcal{F}_t \times_3 J) \times_1 \nu'_t \times_2 y_{t-1}' \right), 0_{N \times 2N} \right)' }

\displaystyle=\norm{\frac{1}{N^2T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \left( \text{mat}_3 \left( (\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_t -\mathcal{F}_t \times_3 J ) \times_1 \nu'_t \times_2 y_{t-1}' \right)' , 0_{N \times 2N} \right)' }

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)\right).

The proof is similar to that of term
a_{2}
defined in part
(i)
, with
\nu_{t}
replacing
X_{t}
.

Finally, consider part
(iii)
.
We have that

\displaystyle\norm{\frac{1}{TN} \sum_{t=1}^T \widehat{X}_{t}'\widehat X_{t} - \frac{1}{TN} \sum_{t=1}^T \bar J X_{t}'X_{t}\bar J }

\displaystyle\leq 2\norm{ \frac{1}{TN} \sum_{t=1}^T (\widehat{X}_{t}' - \bar J X_t') X_{t}\bar J }+\norm{ \frac{1}{TN} \sum_{t=1}^T (\widehat{X}_{t}' - \bar J X_t') (\widehat{X}_{t}' - \bar J X_{t}) }

\displaystyle=I+II.

(.61)

Now,

\displaystyle\norm{I}

\displaystyle=2\norm{
\frac{1}{N^2T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \left( \text{mat}_3 \left( (\widehat{\mathcal{F}}_t -\mathcal{F}_t \times_3 J ) \times_1 \bar J X'_t \times_2 y_{t-1}' \right)', 0_{N \times 2N} \right)'
}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)\right),

following the same steps as in the proof for term
a_{2}
in part
(i)
.
Moreover,
II
is clearly dominated by
I
. This completes the proof.
∎

.7
Proof of Theorem
.1

Proof.

From (
.2
), Proposition
.1
, Assumptions
12
(ii)
and
12
(iii)
, and Slutsky’s theorem:

\displaystyle\sqrt{T}\left(\widehat{\theta}^{\text{\tiny OLS}}-\bar{J}\theta\right)=

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)^{-1}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\nu_{t}\right)+\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}u_{t}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)^{-1}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}\nu_{t}\right)+\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\widehat{X}_{t}-\bar{J}X_{t})^{\prime}\nu_{t}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}u_{t}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}{X}_{t}^{\prime}X_{t}\bar{J}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}\nu_{t}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{T}}{mN^{2-\gamma}}\right)+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{d}{\to}\mathcal{N}\left(0_{r+2},\bar{J}_{0}\Sigma_{XX}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}\Omega_{0}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}\Sigma_{XX}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}\right),

since we assumed
\sqrt{T}/(mN^{2-\gamma})\to 0
and where and
\bar{J}_{0}:=\plim_{m,N,T\to\infty}\bar{J}
. Notice, finally that

\bar{J}_{0}\Sigma_{XX}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}\Omega_{0}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}\Sigma_{XX}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}=\Sigma_{XX}^{-1}\Omega_{0}\Sigma_{XX}^{-1}
. This completes the proof.
∎

.8
Proof of Proposition
.1

Proof.

First of all, notice that from Assumption
6
(vi)
and
by Weyl’s inequality,

\displaystyle\norm{V^{-1}}=\frac{1}{\mu_{N}(V)}\leq\frac{1}{\mu_{N}(\Lambda\Lambda^{\prime})+\mu_{N}(S)}=\frac{1}{\min_{i=1,\ldots,N}\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{it}^{2}]}\leq\frac{1}{\underline{M}_{\epsilon}},

(.62)

where
\mu_{N}(\Lambda\Lambda^{\prime})=0
,
\mu_{N}(V)
, and
\mu_{N}(S)
are the smallest eigenvalues of
\Lambda\Lambda^{\prime}
,
V
, and
S
, respectively, and
\mu_{N}(\Lambda\Lambda^{\prime})=0
.

Consider part
(i)
. We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{V}^{-1}u_{t}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}V^{-1}u_{t}+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}-\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}\right)V^{-1}u_{t}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}(\widehat{V}^{-1}-V^{-1})u_{t}+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}-\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}\right)(\widehat{V}^{-1}-V^{-1})u_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,a+b+c+d.

Because of (
.62
), term
a
behaves like term
a
in (
.52
) in the proof of Proposition
.1
(i)
, thus:

\norm{a}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)\right).

Likewise, term
b
behaves like term
b
in the proof of Proposition
.1
(i)
, thus term
b
is dominated by term
a
. Moreover, because of Lemma
.14
, terms
c
and
d
are dominated by terms
a
and
b
, respectively. This proves part
(i)
.

For part
(ii)
, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{V}^{-1}\nu_{t}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}V^{-1}\nu_{t}+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}(\widehat{V}^{-1}-V^{-1})\nu_{t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}V^{-1}\nu_{t}+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\widehat{X}_{t}-X_{t}\bar{J})^{\prime}V^{-1}\nu_{t}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}(\widehat{V}^{-1}-V^{-1})\nu_{t}+\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\widehat{X}_{t}-X_{t}\bar{J})^{\prime}(\widehat{V}^{-1}-V^{-1})\nu_{t}.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,A+B+C+D.

By Assumption
13
(ii)
:

\norm{A}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\right).

Term
B
, because of (
.62
), behaves like term
B
in (
.60
) in the proof of Proposition
.1
(ii)
, i.e., it behaves like term
A
in part
(i)
:

\norm{B}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)\right).

Terms
C
and
D
are dominated by terms
A
and
B
, respectively, because of Lemma
.14
. This proves part
(ii)
.

For part
(iii)
, we have

\displaystyle\norm{\frac 1{NT}\sum_{t=1}^T \widehat X_t'\widehat V^{-1}\widehat X_t- \frac 1{NT}\sum_{t=1}^T \bar J X_t' V^{-1} X_t\bar J}\leq

\displaystyle\,2\norm{ \frac{1}{TN} \sum_{t=1}^T (\widehat{X}_{t}' - \bar J X_t') V^{-1}X_{t}\bar J }

\displaystyle+\norm{ \frac{1}{TN} \sum_{t=1}^T (\widehat{X}_{t}' - \bar J X_t')V^{-1} (\widehat{X}_{t}' - \bar J X_{t}) }

\displaystyle+\norm{\frac{1}{TN} \sum_{t=1}^T \bar J X_t' (\widehat V^{-1}-V^{-1})X_t\bar J }

\displaystyle+2\norm{\frac{1}{TN} \sum_{t=1}^T (\widehat{X}_{t}' - \bar J X_t')(\widehat V^{-1}-V^{-1})X_t\bar J}

\displaystyle+\norm{\frac{1}{TN} \sum_{t=1}^T (\widehat{X}_{t}' - \bar J X_t')(\widehat V^{-1}-V^{-1})(\widehat{X}_{t}' - \bar J X_t')}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,I+II+III+IV+V.

Because of (
.62
), terms
I
and
II
behave like terms
I
and
II
in (
.61
) in the proof of Proposition
.1
(iii)
, thus

\norm{I}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N^{3-\gamma/2}T},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)\right),

while
II
is dominated by
I
. Then, by Lemma
.14
, we have

\norm{III}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}},\sqrt{\frac{\log N}{T}}\right)\right).

Finally, terms
IV
and
V
are dominated by terms
I
and
II
, respectively, by Lemma
.14
. This completes the proof.
∎

.9
Proof of Theorem
.1

Proof.

From (
.3
), Proposition
.1
, Assumptions
13
(ii)
and
13
(iii)
, and Slutsky’s theorem:

\displaystyle\sqrt{NT}\left(\widehat{\theta}^{\text{\tiny GLS}}-\bar{J}\theta\right)=

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{V}^{-1}\widehat{X}_{t}\right)^{-1}\left\{\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{V}^{-1}\nu_{t}\right)+\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widehat{X}_{t}^{\prime}\widehat{V}^{-1}u_{t}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\left(\frac{1}{NT}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}{X}_{t}^{\prime}{V}^{-1}X_{t}\bar{J}\right)^{-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{NT}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\bar{J}X_{t}^{\prime}{V}^{-1}\nu_{t}\right)

\displaystyle+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{NT}}{mN^{2-\gamma}}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{\sqrt{N}}{\sqrt{m}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{d}{\to}\mathcal{N}\left(0_{r+2},\bar{J}_{0}\Omega_{1}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}\Omega_{1}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}\Omega_{1}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}\right),

since we assumed
\sqrt{{NT}}/(mN^{2-\gamma})\to 0
and
{\sqrt{N}}/{\sqrt{m}N^{1-\gamma/2}}\to 0
and where and
\bar{J}_{0}:=\plim_{m,N,T\to\infty}\bar{J}
. Notice that
\bar{J}_{0}\Omega_{1}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}\Omega_{1}\bar{J}_{0}\bar{J}_{0}\Omega_{1}^{-1}\bar{J}_{0}=\Omega_{1}^{-1}
. This completes the proof.
∎

Auxiliary lemmata

We start with some notation. Let
\chi_{t}:=U\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}
and recall the definition of the
m\times m
matrices:

\displaystyle\widehat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathcal{W}_{(3)t}\mathcal{W}_{(3)t}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\mathcal{W}}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{W}_{(3)t}\mathcal{W}^{\prime}_{(3)t}\right],

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\chi}

\displaystyle:=U\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{(3)t}\right]U^{\prime},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}_{(3)t}\right],

with
j
largest eigenvalues
\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{\mathcal{W}}
,
{\mu}_{j}^{\mathcal{W}}
,
{\mu}_{j}^{\chi}
, and
{\mu}_{j}^{\mathcal{E}}
, respectively.

Lemma .1
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
:

(i)

for all
j=1,\dots,r
,

\underline{C}_{j}<\underset{m,N\to\infty}{\text{lim inf }}\frac{\mu_{j}^{\chi}}{mN^{2}}\leq\underset{m,N\to\infty}{\text{lim sup }}\frac{\mu_{j}^{\chi}}{mN^{2}}<\overline{C}_{j}

for some finite
\underline{C}_{j}
and
\overline{C}_{j}
independent of
m
and
N
.

(ii)

For all
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
and

T\in\mathbb{N}
,

\frac{1}{mN^{\gamma}T}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\sum_{h,k=1}^{N^{2}}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}\absolutevalue{
\mathbb{E} \left[ \mathcal{E}_{(3) t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3) s j k} \right]
}\leq M_{2\mathcal{E}}

for some finite
M_{2\mathcal{E}}
independent of
m,N,T
and some
\gamma\in[0,2]
.

(iii)

For all
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\frac{1}{mN^{\gamma}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\sum_{h,k=1}^{N^{2}}\absolutevalue{
\mathbb{E} \left[ \mathcal{E}_{(3) t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3) s j k} \right]
}\leq M_{3\mathcal{E}}

for some finite
M_{3\mathcal{E}}
independent of
m,N,T
and some
\gamma\in[0,2]
.

(iv)

Let
\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{E}}
denote the largest eigenvalue of
\Gamma^{\mathcal{E}}
.
Then,
\frac{\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{E}}}{N^{\gamma}}\leq M_{\mathcal{E}}
for some
\gamma\in[0,2]
and some finite
M_{\mathcal{E}}
.

Proof.

For part
(i)
, by
Merikoski and Kumar 2004
(Theorem 7), for all
j=1,\dots,r
, we have

\frac{\mu_{r}(U^{\prime}U)}{m}\frac{\mu_{j}(\Gamma^{\mathcal{F}})}{N^{2}}\leq\frac{\mu_{j}^{\chi}}{mN^{2}}\leq\frac{\mu_{j}(U^{\prime}U)}{m}\frac{\mu_{1}(\Gamma^{\mathcal{F}})}{N^{2}},

where
\Gamma^{\mathcal{F}}=\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}^{\prime}\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}]
and
\mu_{j}(\cdot)
denotes the
j
-th eigenvalues of the matrix in parenthesis.
The proof follows from Assumption
1
(i)
which, by continuity of the eigenvalues, implies that, for any
j=1,\dots,r
, as
m\to\infty

\lim_{m\to\infty}\frac{\mu_{j}(U^{\prime}U)}{m}=\mu_{j}(\Sigma_{U}),

with

0<m_{U}^{2}\leq\mu_{r}(\Sigma_{U})\leq\mu_{1}(\Sigma_{U})\leq M^{2}_{U}<\infty,

and by Assumption
1
(ii)
, which implies that

\frac{\mu_{r}(\Gamma^{\mathcal{F}})}{N^{2}}
and
\frac{\mu_{1}(\Gamma^{\mathcal{F}})}{N^{2}}
are both finite and bounded away from zero.

For part
(ii)
, by Assumption
2
(ii)
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{mN^{\gamma}T}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\sum_{h,k=1}^{N^{2}}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}\absolutevalue{
\mathbb{E} \left[ \mathcal{E}_{(3) t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3) s j k} \right]
}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\sum_{h,k=1}^{N^{2}}\sum_{l=-(T-1)}^{T-1}\left(1-\frac{\absolutevalue{l}}{T}\right)\frac{\absolutevalue{
\mathbb{E} \left[ \mathcal{E}_{(3) t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3) s j k} \right] }}{N^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{i=1,\dots,m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}\rho_{\mathcal{E}}^{\absolutevalue{l}}M_{ij}\leq\frac{M_{\mathcal{E}}(1+\rho_{\mathcal{E}})}{1-\rho_{\mathcal{E}}}.

Similarly, for part
(iii)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{mN^{\gamma}}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\sum_{h,k=1}^{N^{2}}\absolutevalue{
\mathbb{E} \left[ \mathcal{E}_{(3) t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3) t j k} \right]
}\leq

\displaystyle\max_{i=1,\dots,m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}M_{ij}\leq M_{\mathcal{E}}.

For part
(iv)
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\gamma}}\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{E}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\gamma}}\norm{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3) t} \mathcal{E}_{(3) t}' \right]}

(.1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\gamma}}\max_{i=1,\dots,m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\absolutevalue{\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3) t i \cdot}' \mathcal{E}_{(3) t \cdot j}' \right] }

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\gamma}}\max_{i=1,\dots,m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\absolutevalue{ \sum_{h=1}^{N^2} \mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3) t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3) t h j}' \right] }

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{\gamma}}\max_{i=1,\dots,m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{h=1}^{N^{2}}\absolutevalue{ \mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3) t i h} \mathcal{E}_{(3) t h j}' \right] }

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle M_{\mathcal{E}},

following from Assumption
2
(ii)

∎

Lemma .2
.

Under Assumption
3
,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{\gamma/2}}\norm{ \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}' }=O_{p}(1).

Proof.

From Assumption
3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{mTN^{\gamma}}\norm{ \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t }' }^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{mTN^{\gamma}}\norm{ \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t }' }^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{mN^{\gamma}T}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\norm{ \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t i\cdot}^\prime}^{2}\right]\leq C_{\mathcal{FE}}.

∎

Lemma .3
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
4
,

(i)

U=V^{\chi}(M^{\chi})^{1/2}N^{-1}
.

(ii)

\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}=N(M^{\chi})^{-\frac{1}{2}}V^{\chi}{}^{\prime}\chi_{t}
.

where
V^{\chi}
is the
m\times r
matrix whose
j
-th column is the normalized eigenvector corresponding to the
j
-th largest eigenvalue
\mu_{j}^{\chi}
of the matrix
\Gamma^{\chi}=E(\chi_{t}\chi_{t}^{\prime})
,
and
M^{\chi}
is the
r\times r
diagonal matrix with
\mu_{j}^{\chi}
as its entry
(j,j)
.

Proof.

For part
(i)
,
Assumption
1
(i)
implies
\frac{\Gamma^{\chi}}{N^{2}}=UU^{\prime}
. Therefore, since the non-zero eigenvalues of
\frac{\Gamma^{\chi}}{mN^{2}}
are the same as the
r
eigenvalues of
\frac{U^{\prime}U}{m}
, which is diagonal by Assumption
4
(i)
.
Then, we must have, for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

\frac{U^{\prime}U}{m}=\frac{M^{\chi}}{mN^{2}}.

Since
\Gamma^{\chi}=V^{\chi}M^{\chi}V^{\chi}{}^{\prime}
, it must be that

UK=\frac{V^{\chi}(M^{\chi})^{1/2}}{N},

for some
r\times r
invertible
K
. By multiplying on the right both sides by their transposed:

K^{\prime}U^{\prime}UK=\frac{M^{\chi}}{N^{2}},

since eigenvectors are normalized. Thus, we must have
K=I_{r}
.
This proves part
(i)
.

For part
(ii)
, since
\chi=U\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}
, then by linear projection of
U
onto
\chi_{t}
, and using part (i), for
t=1,\dots,T
,

\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}=\left(U^{\prime}U\right)^{-1}U^{\prime}\chi_{t}=N(M^{\chi})^{-\frac{1}{2}}V^{\chi}{}^{\prime}\chi_{t}.

This proves part
(ii)
. ∎

Lemma .4
.

Under Assumptions
1
and
2
, for all
t=1,\dots,T
and all
m,N,T\in\mathbb{N}
,

(i)

\norm{ \frac{U}{\sqrt{m}} }=O(1)
.

(ii)

\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}}{N}}=O_{p}(1)
and

\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)}}{\sqrt{T}N}}=O_{p}(1)
.

(iii)

\norm{\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{\sqrt{m}N}}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)
and

\norm{\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)}}{\sqrt{mT}N}}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N^{1-\gamma/2}}\right)
.

Proof.

By Assumption
1
(i)
, which holds for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\norm{\frac{U}{\sqrt{m}}}^{2}\leq\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\norm{\frac{U}{\sqrt{m}}}^{2}_{F}=\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{i=1}^{m}u_{ij}^{2}\leq\sup_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{i=1,\dots,m}\norm{u_i}^{2}\leq M_{U}^{2},

since
M_{U}
is independent of
i
. This proves part
(i)
.

For part
(ii)
, by Assumption
1
(ii)

\displaystyle\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\dots,T}\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}}{N}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\dots,T}\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}}{N}}^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\dots,T}\mathbb{E}\left[\tr\left(\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}^{\prime}}{N^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\dots,T}\tr( \frac{1}{N^2} \mathbb{E} \left[ \mathcal{F}_{(3)t}\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}' \right])=r.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)}}{\sqrt{T}N}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)}}{\sqrt{T}N}}^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}}{N}}^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\dots,T}\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{F}_{(3)t}}{N}}^{2}_{F}\right]=r.

For part
(iii)
, by Lemma
.1
(iv)

\displaystyle\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\dots,T}\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{\sqrt{m}N}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\dots,T}\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{\sqrt{m}N}}^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\dots,T}\mathbb{E}\left[\tr\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}^{\prime}}{mN^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\dots,T}\frac{1}{mN^{2}}\tr( \Gamma^{\mathcal{E}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{\mathcal{E}}N^{\gamma}}{N^{2-\gamma}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)}}{\sqrt{mT}N}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)}}{\sqrt{mT}N}}^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{\sqrt{m}N}}^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\sup_{N,T\in\mathbb{N}}\max_{t=1,\dots,T}\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{\frac{\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}}{\sqrt{m}N}}^{2}_{F}\right]\leq\frac{M_{\mathcal{E}}N^{\gamma}}{N^{2-\gamma}},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma .5
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
2
, for all
m,N,T\in\mathbb{N}
,

(i)

\norm{ \frac{1}{N^2 T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t}' - \frac{\Gamma^{\mathcal{F}}}{N^2}}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\right).

(ii)

\norm{ \frac{1}{ m N^2 T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}\mathcal{E}_{(3)t}' - \frac{\Gamma^{\mathcal{E}}}{m N^2} }=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N^{2-\gamma/2}\sqrt{T}}\right).

Proof.

For part
(i)
, because of Assumption
1
(iv)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{ \frac{1}{N^2 T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t}' - \frac{\Gamma^{\mathcal{F}}}{N^2}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{ \frac{1}{N^2 T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t}' - \frac{\Gamma^{\mathcal{F}}}{N^2}}^{2}_{F}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N^{2}T}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\{\mathcal{F}_{(3)tj\cdot}\mathcal{F}_{(3)ti\cdot}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{F}_{(3)tj\cdot}\mathcal{F}_{(3)ti\cdot}^{\prime}]\}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r^{2}C_{\mathcal{F}}}{N^{2}T}.

This proves part
(i)
.

For part
(ii)
,
because of Assumption
2
(v)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{ \frac{1}{m N^2 T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}' - \frac{\Gamma^{\mathcal{E}}}{m N^2}}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{mN^{4-\gamma}T}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{\gamma/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{\mathcal{E}_{(3)ti\cdot}\mathcal{E}_{(3)tj\cdot}^{\prime}-\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}_{(3)ti\cdot}\mathcal{E}_{(3)tj\cdot}^{\prime}\right]\right\}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{\mathcal{E}}}{N^{4-\gamma}T},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma .6
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
4
, for all
m,T,N\in\mathbb{N}

(i)

\frac{1}{mN^{2}}\norm{ \widehat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}} - \Gamma^{\mathcal{W}} }=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\right)
.

(ii)

\frac{1}{mN^{2}}\norm{ \widehat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}} - \Gamma^{\chi} }=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{N^{2-\gamma}m}\right)\right)
.

(iii)

\frac{1}{mN^{2}}\left|\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{\mathcal{W}}-\mu_{j}^{\chi}\right|=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{N^{2-\gamma}m}\right)\right)
.

for all
j

(iv)

\norm{\widehat{V}^{\mathcal{W}} - V^{\chi} J}=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{N^{2-\gamma}m}\right)\right)
,

where
\widehat{V}^{\mathcal{W}}
is the
m\times r
matrix whose
j
-th column is the normalized (unit-modulus) eigenvector corresponding to the
j
-th largest eigenvalue of
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}}
,
V^{\chi}
is the
m\times r
matrix whose
j
-th column is the normalized eigenvector corresponding to the
j
-th largest eigenvalue of
\Gamma^{\chi}
, and
J
is a
r\times r
diagonal matrix whose diagonal entries are
\pm 1
.

Proof.

For part
(i)
, we have that

\displaystyle\norm{\frac{1}{m N^2} \left( \widehat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}} - \Gamma^{\mathcal{W}} \right) }

\displaystyle\leq\norm{
\frac{1}{mN^2}
\left\{
U \left( \frac{1}{T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t}' - \Gamma^{\mathcal{F}} \right) U'
+ \frac{1}{T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}' - \Gamma^{\mathcal{E}}
\right\} }

\displaystyle+\norm{\frac{2}{mN^2 T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}^\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\norm{ \frac{U}{\sqrt{m}} }^{2}\norm{ \frac{1}{T N^2} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t}' - \frac{\Gamma^{\mathcal{F}}}{N^2}}+\norm{ \left( \frac{1}{m T N^2} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}' - \frac{\Gamma^{\mathcal{E}} }{m N^2}\right) }

\displaystyle+\norm{\frac{2}{mN^2 T} \sum_{t=1}^{T} \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}^\prime}.

By Lemma
.4
(i)
,
.5
(i)
and
.5
(ii)
, the first two terms are

O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\right)
.
By Lemma
.2
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{m^{2}N^{4}T^{2}}\norm{ \sum_{t=1}^T \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}^\prime}^{2}\right]=O\left(\frac{1}{mTN^{4-\gamma}}\right),

so

\norm{ \frac 1{m N^2 T} \sum_{t=1}^T \mathcal{F}_{(3)t} \mathcal{E}_{(3)t}^\prime}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{mT}N^{2-\gamma/2}}\right).

This proves part
(i)
.

For part
(ii)
, under Assumption
3
, we have that

\widehat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}}-\Gamma^{\chi}=\widehat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}}-\Gamma^{\mathcal{W}}+\Gamma^{\mathcal{E}}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{mN^{2}}\norm{ \widehat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}} - \Gamma^{\chi} }

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{mN^{2}}\norm{ \widehat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}} - \Gamma^{\mathcal{W}} }+\frac{1}{mN^{2}}\norm{ \Gamma^{\mathcal{E}} }

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}}\right)+O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}}\right),

following from part (i) and Lemma
.1
(iv)
, since
\norm{\Gamma^{\mathcal{E}}}
is bounded by
\mu_{1}^{\mathcal{E}}
.

For part
(iii)
, given Weyl’s inequality,

\left|\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{\mathcal{W}}-\mu_{j}^{\chi}\right|\leq\mu_{1}\left(\widehat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}}-\Gamma^{\chi}\right)=\norm{ \widehat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}} - \Gamma^{\chi} },

so, given part
(ii)
,

\frac{1}{mN^{2}}\left|\widehat{\mu}_{j}^{\mathcal{W}}-\mu_{j}^{\chi}\right|=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}}\right)\right).

Last, for part
(iv)
, given part
(i)
, Lemma
.1
(i)
, and Theorem 2 in
Yu et al. 2015
, which is a special case of Davis-Kahn Theorem

\norm{\widehat{V}^{\mathcal{W}} - V^{\chi} J}\leq\frac{\norm{ \widehat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}} - \Gamma^{\chi} }}{\mu^{\chi}_{r}}=\frac{N^{2}mO_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{N^{2-\gamma}m}\right)}{O(N^{2}m)}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{N^{2-\gamma}m}\right),

and this completes the proof.
∎

Lemma .7
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
4
, for all
m,T,N\in\mathbb{N}

(i)

\norm{ \frac{M^{\chi}}{m N^2}}=O(1)
.

(ii)

\norm{ \left(\frac{M^{\chi}}{m N^2} \right)^{-1}}=O(1)
.

(iii)

\norm{ \frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{m N^2}}=O_{p}(1)
.

(iv)

\norm{ \left( \frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{m N^2} \right)^{-1}}=O_{p}(1)
.

Proof.

Parts
(i)
and
(ii)
follow directly from Lemma
.1
, indeed,

\norm{\frac{M^{\chi}}{m N^2}}=\frac{\mu_{1}^{\chi}}{mN^{2}}\leq\overline{C}_{1},

and

\norm{ \left( \frac{M^{\chi}}{m N^2} \right)}=\frac{mN^{2}}{\mu_{r}^{\chi}}\leq\underline{C}_{r}.

Both statements hold for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
since the eigenvalues are an increasing sequence in
m
and
N
.

For part
(iii)
, because of Lemma
.6
(iii)
,

\norm{ \frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{m N^2}}\leq\norm{ \frac{M^{\chi}}{m N^2} }+\norm{ \frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{m N^2} - \frac{M^{\chi}}{m N^2} }\leq\overline{C}_{1}+O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{N^{2-\gamma}m}\right)\right).

For part
(iv)
, just notice that, because of Lemma
.6
(iii)
and part
(ii)
, then
\frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{mN^{2}}
is positive definite with probability tending to one as
m,N,T\to\infty
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma .8
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
4
, for any given
i=1,\ldots,m
and

m,T\to\infty

(i)

\frac{1}{mN^{2}}\norm{\varepsilon_i' \left(\widehat{\Gamma}^{\mathcal{W}} - \Gamma^{\chi}\right) }=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{2-\gamma}m}}\right)\right)
.

(ii)

\norm{\sqrt{m} v_i^\chi}=O_{p}(1)
.

(iii)

\norm{\sqrt{m}\, \widehat v_i^{\mathcal W'}-\sqrt{m} v_i^{\chi'}J }=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{N^{2-\gamma}m}\right)
.

Proof.

Part
(i)
follows directly from Lemma
.6
(ii)
.
For part
(ii)
notice that for all
i=1,\ldots,m
, since
N^{-2}\Gamma^{\mathcal{F}}=I_{r}
because of Assumption
4
(ii)
, we must have

\frac{\text{Var}(\chi_{it})}{N^{2}}=u_{i}^{\prime}u_{i}\leq M_{U}^{2},

(.2)

which is finite for all
i
and
t
. Then, by Lemma
.1
(i)

\displaystyle\lim\inf_{m\to\infty}\max_{i=1,\ldots,m}\text{Var}(\chi_{it})

\displaystyle=\lim\inf_{m\to\infty}\max_{i=1,\ldots,m}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\mu_{j}^{\chi}\absolutevalue{V_{ij}^\chi}^{2}\geq\lim\inf_{m\to\infty}\max_{i=1,\ldots,m}\mu_{r}^{\chi}\sum_{j=1}^{r}\absolutevalue{V_{ij}^\chi}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\underline{C}_{r}mN^{2}\max_{i=1,\ldots,m}\norm{v_i^\chi}^{2},

and by (
.2
) we must have

\underline{C}_{r}mN^{2}\max_{i=1,\ldots,m}\norm{v_i^\chi}^{2}\leq M_{U}^{2}N^{2},

which implies

m\max_{i=1,\ldots,m}\norm{v_i^\chi}^{2}\leq\frac{M_{U}^{2}}{\underline{C}_{r}}.

This proves part
(ii)
.

For part
(iii)
we follow the same approach as in the proof of Lemma
.6
(iv)
but using part
(i)
.

∎

Lemma .9
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
4
, for
m,T\to\infty

(i)

\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\norm{ \widehat{U} - U J }=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{N^{2-\gamma}m}\right)\right)
.

(ii)

for any given
i=1,\ldots,m
,
\norm{ \widehat{u_i}' - u_i' J }=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{2-\gamma}m}}\right)\right)
.

Proof.

, first notice that
\text{rk}\left(\frac{U}{\sqrt{m}}\right)=r
for all

, since
\text{rk}\left(\frac{\Gamma^{\mathcal{F}}}{N^{2}}\right)=r
by Assumption
1
(ii)
and
\text{rk}\left(\frac{\Gamma^{\chi}}{m}\right)=r
by Lemma
.1
(i)
.
Indeed,

\text{rk}\left(\frac{\Gamma^{\mathcal{F}}}{mN^{2}}\right)\leq\min\left(\text{rk}\left(\frac{\Gamma^{\mathcal{F}}}{N^{2}}\right),\text{rk}\left(\frac{U}{\sqrt{m}}\right)\right)
.
This holds for all
m>\overline{m}
and since eigenvalues are an increasing sequence in
m
.
Therefore,
\left(\frac{U^{\prime}U}{m}\right)^{-1}
is well defined for all
m
and
U
admits a left inverse.

Now, because of Lemma
.6
(iii)
,
.6
(iv)
,
.7
(i), using (
4.4
)

\displaystyle\norm{ \frac{\widehat{U} - U J}{\sqrt{m}} }

\displaystyle=\norm{\widehat{V}^{\mathcal{W}} \left( \frac{\widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}}}{m N^2} \right)^{1/2}
-
V^{\chi} \left( \frac{M^{\chi}}{m N^2} \right)^{1/2} J }

\displaystyle\leq\norm{\widehat{V}^{\mathcal{W}} - V^{\chi} J}\norm{\frac{M^{\chi}}{m N^2}}+\norm{ \frac{1}{\sqrt{m} N} \left\{ \left( \widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}} \right)^{1/2} - \left( M^{\chi} \right)^{1/2} \right\} }\norm{V^{\chi}}

\displaystyle+\norm{\widehat{V}^{\mathcal{W}} - V^{\chi} J}\norm{ \frac{1}{\sqrt{m} N} \left\{ \left( \widehat{M}^{\mathcal{W}} \right)^{1/2} - \left( M^{\chi} \right)^{1/2} \right\} }

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{mN^{2-\gamma}}\right)\right).

For part
(ii)
, because of Lemmas

.6
(iii)
,

.7
(iii)
,

.8
(ii)
, and

.8
(iii)

\displaystyle\norm{\widehat{u}_i'-u_i'J}

\displaystyle=\norm{\sqrt{m}\, \widehat v_i^{\mathcal W'}\left(\frac{\widehat M^{\mathcal W}}{mN^2}\right)^{1/2}-\sqrt{m} v_i^{\chi'}J
\left(\frac{M^\chi}{mN^2}\right)^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq\norm{\sqrt{m}\, \widehat v_i^{\mathcal W'}-\sqrt{m} v_i^{\chi'}J }\,\norm{\frac{\widehat M^{\mathcal W}}{mN^2}}+\norm{\frac 1{\sqrt{m}} \left\{\left(\widehat M^{\mathcal W}\right)^{1/2}-\left( M^{\chi}\right)^{1/2}\right\} }\,\norm{\sqrt{m} v_i^\chi}

\displaystyle+\norm{\sqrt{m}\, \widehat v_i^{\mathcal W'}-\sqrt{m} v_i^{\chi'}J }\,\norm{\frac 1{\sqrt{mN^2}} \left\{\left(\widehat M^{\mathcal W}\right)^{1/2}-\left( M^{\chi}\right)^{1/2}\right\} }

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\max\left(\frac{1}{N\sqrt{T}},\frac{1}{\sqrt{mN^{2-\gamma}}}\right)\right).
